# Script Catalog

This catalog is generated from `ScriptInfo.json` files in each script folder.

To update it after changing script metadata, run:

```powershell
.\tools\Update-ScriptCatalog.ps1
```

## Status Legend

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Example | Working example intended for customization |
| Template | Starting point for a new script |
| Planned | Placeholder for a future contribution |

## Risk Legend

| Risk | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Low | Read-only, reporting, or simple additive change |
| Medium | Changes device/user configuration and should be piloted |
| High | Changes security state, deletes data, changes trust, or can remove access |

## Detection Evidence Legend

| Evidence Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| DirectEvidence | Detection reads a recognized device evidence source directly |
| SnapshotFreshness | Detection validates that a snapshot file exists and is current |
| PackageMarker | Win32 detection uses a package-owned install/version marker |
| MarkerOnly | Detection only reads repository-managed marker state and needs review before being treated as real device-state compliance |
| NeedsReview | The audit could not identify a strong evidence source |
| N/A | The workload does not use a detection script contract |

## Portability Legend

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Portability Risk | Highest static audit risk: None, Low, Medium, or High |
| Portability Review | Reviewed, NeedsReview, or NotApplicable |
| Portability Areas | Localization, OsVersion, CommandParsing, Scalability, RegistryView, or PathAssumption findings |

| Disk Detect Predictive Failure | Detection and Remediation | Endpoint-Health | Example | System | Recommended | Yes | No | Yes | File system | No | Medium | DirectEvidence | Reviewed | CIM/WMI; Storage | Low | Reviewed | Localization | Static audit found 1 portability finding(s): Localization. | This remediation package detects predictive failure signals for the disk where Windows is installed. The detection script resolves the Windows/system drive, normally `C:`, to its backing physical disk and checks only that disk. It checks SMART predictive failure data from `MSStorageDriver_FailurePredictStatus` and can optionally use Windows disk health as a fallback. The remediation script does not try to repair hardware. It logs the finding, optionally sends a Teams alert, and keeps Intune reporting the issue until an admin takes action. | `Detection-Remediation/Endpoint-Health/Disk-Detect-Predictive-Failure` |

## Recommended Folder Pattern

Each script should live in its own folder and include:

```text
<Workload>/
`-- <Purpose-Category>/
    `-- <Script-Folder-Name>/
        |-- ScriptInfo.json
        |-- README.md
        |-- <Required script files>
        `-- <Optional supporting files>
```

## Adding a New Script

1. Pick the correct workload.
2. Pick the correct purpose category.
3. Run `tools\New-IntuneScriptFolder.ps1` or copy the nearest example.
4. Keep the script self-contained.
5. Put all editable values in the `CONFIGURATION` section.
6. Update `ScriptInfo.json` and `README.md`.
7. Run `tools\Update-ScriptCatalog.ps1`.
8. Run `tools\Test-DetectionEvidence.ps1 -UpdateScriptInfo` when detection logic changes.
9. Run `tools\Test-ScriptPortability.ps1 -UpdateScriptInfo` when script logic changes.
10. Run `tools\Test-Repository.ps1` before publishing.

## Generator Example

```powershell
.\tools\New-IntuneScriptFolder.ps1 `
    -Workload Detection-Remediation `
    -ScriptCategory Security `
    -Name Defender-Example-Recommendation `
    -Summary 'Detects and remediates an example Defender recommendation.' `
    -Context System `
    -Requires64Bit `
    -WritesTo 'Defender preference' `
    -Risk High `
    -IncludeTeamsAlertBlock
```

## Defender Secure Score Style Scripts

For Microsoft Defender Secure Score recommendations that require registry enforcement, use `Detection-Remediation/Security` unless the setting needs app-like install/uninstall behavior.

Recommended structure:

```text
Detection-Remediation/
`-- Security/
    `-- Defender-<Recommendation-Short-Name>/
        |-- ScriptInfo.json
        |-- Detect.ps1
        |-- Remediate.ps1
        `-- README.md
```

Use a folder per recommendation. This keeps Intune assignments, reporting, and troubleshooting clean.
